#7487. Google’s Q3 2026 Earnings Call: Search & AI Are Merging Into One Unified Product via @sejournal, @marie_haynes

See how Search Engine Journal checks a real site for crawler access and ranks fixes by business impact.

AI Mode, AI Overviews, and traditional Search are becoming one product. What Google’s Q3 earnings call revealed about the agentic era.

Even though Wall Street is panicking over Google’s earnings call, it seems obvious to me what is happening. They are not going to slow down when it comes to AI. Throughout the call, they spoke of agents and how we are in the early days of how they are changing Search. They told us that AI Mode, AI overviews, and traditional Search are becoming one unified product.

They also said to expect rapid improvement because of Recursive Self-Improvement (RSI). Things are about to get very interesting.

I think new opportunities are arising, but also much of the way we have traditionally been able to make money from Search is disappearing.

If you prefer video, I share my thoughts on the call.

The main thing I took away from this earnings call is the emphasis on the shift to agentic search. I think this is still unfolding and no one fully knows what this new era of the web will look like. Google gave us a few hints in this call.

I find it fascinating how Google’s stock plummeted after this call. Alphabet’s revenues were up 25% YOY and Cloud revenue was up 103%. The general concern seems to be that Google is pushing AI Mode more, and so far there are fewer opportunities for ads shown in AI Mode. Therefore, revenue from Search is lower than expected. Still, Search advertising is up 12% YOY. (Wall Street was expecting 15%.)

I have no concerns over Google’s ability to continue to thrive. They mentioned in the call that their first priority is to make AI Mode a quality product and then it will focus more on monetizing it. Last week, Google announced that ads in AI Mode were expanding to more countries including Australia, Canada and more.

“Our approach is deliberate – prioritizing the user experience and engagement first, then scaling the formats.”

Mark my word, Google will make plenty of money from ads in the years to come. I think the new connected apps (see more on this below) could eventually be offered as ads.

They also spoke extensively about how there is incredible demand for Google’s computing power. It talked about TPUs not as chip sales, but as systems. “TPU Systems” were mentioned 35 times in the call. Thomas Kurian said:

“A TPU System is not just a chip, but it’s a specialized system that combines multiple things, including hardware, computer, storage, networking and so we are selling much more than just a chip. Software, training, inference.”

I mention this because Google will likely see incredible growth as more and more customers purchase these TPU systems.

What’s most interesting to me is how Google’s verbiage has shifted when it comes to talking about AI Mode and its relationship to Search.

The idea of traditional Search and AI Search merging into one product was mentioned several times:

“We are building one seamless experience across AI Overviews, AI Mode, and Traditional Search.”

“We think of Search holistically, a unified product spanning traditional results and AI powered Search.

I know it feels like Google is behind when it comes to having an agentic product. X has Grokbot. OpenAI has Dots, and Meta’s Muse is taking off. It seems clear to me that the vision of AI being our personal assistant has been in Google’s mind for some time. I think they’re taking their time to fully unpack it all because they need to get it right. Google’s end game here is not just an agent that will help us get things done online, but much bigger!

In May of 2025, Demis Hassabis wrote about Google’s dream of building what they called a “Universal AI Assistant.” Much of this piece discussed Google’s Genie world models acting as training environments for robots and how Project Astra which is now in Gemini Live, will allow us to have insight on the world around us. Google’s mission to organize the world’s information and make it universally accessible and useful.

I see the pieces coming together. It starts with online agents and will eventually expand into every aspect of our lives especially as the technology develops for intelligent eyewear and in-home robots.

“We are in the early stages of agentic experiences across our surfaces, and excited about the commercial opportunities ahead.”

He went on to speak about how they started by building agentic experiences into Gemini Enterprise first and, “as a next step [they’re] bringing these features to Search.”

“It’s clear that the agentic capabilities we all envision are going to be real.”

“I’m excited about agents, and I would view it as a new frontier, which brings in a new set of use cases to Search.”

Those new use cases likely start with Connected Apps.

By the way, if you want to be ahead of the curve in seeing how businesses are adapting to agentic search, I send out an email every week with all of the latest news on Search & AI:

On the day of the call, Google announced the Connected Apps Platform. There are already a handful of apps that can be used via MCP by Gemini either in the app or from within Search. Here is an example they gave where a user calls @Peloton to bring the Peloton app experience into their agentic flow within Gemini.

You can apply to the Connected Apps Platform here.

It was six months ago that Sundar Pichai said in an interview that “Search will be an agent manager.” I think this will be a new era where we shift from Search being a place to find websites that have information to a place where you use tools to help you (or your agents) get things done.

“The Gemini app is now at one billion users and is the fastest growing product in Google’s History.”

A billion users is the same number reported by ChatGPT in June of 2026.

I find this fascinating because my own use of the Gemini app has declined significantly. I use Gemini in Antigravity for almost everything.

We all know that Google’s models have not been as advanced as those from OpenAI and Anthropic.

Last week, Google announced a new frontier model, just two weeks after Gemini 4 Argon. Gemini 4 Carbon is currently undergoing voluntary testing as per the U.S. Government’s recommendations and will soon be released. Artificial Analysis currently has Carbon listed as the most intelligent model to date.

On the call, Sundar Pichai said that more models are coming soon, including “more efficient versions to help developers optimize for speed and cost.”

He said they started seeing a step change with Gemini 3.8 Flash when it came to coding internally at Google. And then Gemini 4 Argon was a “huge point of inflection.” He said:

“It is one of the most meaningful internal productivity levers [they] have seen on software engineering, directly tracking high-quality code that’s getting checked in, and the rate at which it’s getting checked in.”

“Carbon is a meaningful step up from Argon.”

Right now I use Gemini 4 Argon across three or four simultaneous Antigravity conversations most of the day long. On the Ultra plan, I have barely touched my quota. I thought 3.8 Flash was amazing. It helped me start to automate many of the SEO reports I run. When I switched to Gemini 4 Argon a mind blowing thing happened. Gemini 4 Argon + my internal OKF powered knowledge brain and my algo update MCP started producing far better consulting reports than I could alone. I truly believe we are not far from the day where AI will be better at all knowledge work than even the best humans in their field.

Last week, Google announced Genie 4, its latest world model. While it’s cool to see how it can generate interesting scenes and games, the most exciting thing about this model is that it will be used to create training simulations for AI agents and also for robots.

“Beyond our Gemini 4 series, yesterday we announced Genie 4, our most advanced world model yet that simulates safe, realistic, interactive worlds with native audio in real time. This is an important building block for training robots and agents.”

“I would expect the next twelve months to see continued acceleration because of RSI.”

Buckle up. RSI is “Recursive Self Improvement.” AI is being used more and more to help improve AI. We will see it improve rapidly.

Here’s the call if you’d like to listen to it.

#7485. Platform Engineer (remote work) at CloudLinux

CloudLinux builds Linux infrastructure and security products. You will join our Automation & Management Services cell, working closely with Dmitrii Petrov to solve problems across teams and services: cloud-cost data, infrastructure inventory, network policies and capacity workflows.

Check out our website for more information https://cloudlinux.com/

Inside the Infrastructure Department, the Platform cell is a small team. We run the observability platform, the company's GitLab and the CI runners behind it, a few smaller engineering services, and the automation the department relies on for provisioning and configuration.

We are looking for a Platform Engineer for the PaaS team to take ownership of an agreed set of platform services: keep them reliable and within agreed service levels, make changes and recovery repeatable, and reduce recurring operational work through automation and self-service.

You get real freedom in how you implement things, and you own the result: you pick the approach, defend it in review, and answer for how the service behaves afterwards.

Most of the job is what site reliability engineering is about: services that run well day after day, and requests from product teams handled properly. Some of it is building. The observability platform and the runner cluster were both built from scratch within the last year, and there will be more of that. Expect the work to split between platform engineering, incident response, and helping engineers in other teams use what we run.

You do not need to have run every system on this list. Solid fundamentals and the judgment to pick up an unfamiliar service, make it observable and hand back a runbook matter more than matching every line.

#7484. Reddit pitches its 1,300 gaming-related communities to brands

The company said that in-app conversations “created and shaped by real players” can drive engagement and influence purchasing decisions.

Reddit shared new data on its evolving in-app gaming conversation with a playbook for advertisers that are seeking to get their promotions in front of gamers and gaming-related audiences.

Based on research conducted by TransUnion, Reddit said that gaming marketers are largely failing to capitalize on the opportunities afforded by its highly engaged communities.

As explained by Reddit: “Reddit is home to more than 1,300 gaming-related communities, where conversations are created and shaped by real players. These communities don't just bring gamers together; they influence discovery, engagement, and even purchasing decisions.”

Underlining this, Reddit shared additional notes on gaming engagement, and what that means for marketing approaches.

First, according to comparative analysis of Reddit promotions, Reddit ads drove an average of $8.58 return-on-ad-spend for gaming advertisers.

The analysis also found that Reddit discussions and engagement led to broader halo effect across other channels, boosting overall interest in the advertised products.

As explained by Reddit: “This means Reddit’s modeled contribution included value beyond its own platform, demonstrating how Reddit can complement other media touchpoints and contribute to broader marketing performance. TV accounted for 18% of Reddit’s total synergy, followed by paid search at 17%, online video at 15%, and paid social at 12%.”

In other words, Reddit’s gaming communities are influential, which can expand the value of in-app promotions.

On the basis of the last point, the analysis suggested that gaming brands have significantly underinvested in Reddit ads, with Reddit representing just 5% of paid social investment, on average.

Of course, that’s assuming that these same trends would hold true if more gaming ads were present in the app. But based on the data included in the analysis, there might be more opportunity and potential for gaming marketers there.

The report also said that brands that ran a full-funnel strategy across key campaign objectives saw 19% stronger overall ROAS than the single-tactic average. The report added that video views delivered the strongest ROAS.

There are some interesting data points here, though the parameters of the study make it impossible to establish a true like-for-like comparison in relative ad spend.

But the indications suggest that Reddit ads could deliver value and unique audience reach for gaming marketers.

#7480. Meta ends support for Quest 2 and Quest Pro

Meta is shifting focus to its next-gen VR headset, which will be launched next year.

Meta announced that it will end feature development for its Quest 2 and Quest Pro VR devices by March 2027 as the company looks to the next stage of virtual reality development. The announcement comes ahead of the launch of its Meta VR glasses, which are set to launch next spring.

Meta’s Quest 2 device was launched in 2022, which was shortly after the company announced a broad-ranging metaverse vision that was centered around VR connection.

The Quest Pro was the evolution of this and included more advanced technology to enhance the VR experience.

Both devices, however, became less of a priority for the company over time as Meta shifted its attention to generative artificial intelligence. As a result, Quest headsets became something of an afterthought, with Meta now putting an end date on its support of its once-lauded device.

Meta said that feature development for both devices will end with v213, which will reach all devices in March.

“New OS features, platform capabilities, and APIs released after v213 will only be available on Quest 3, Quest 3S, and newer devices,” Meta said. “After v213, we’ll keep shipping security patches and critical bug fixes through the end of 2027.”

Meta noted that apps that support Quest 2 and Quest Pro will still be submittable through December 2027, which will be the final cut-off date for Quest 2 support.

That will leave Quest 3, which Meta launched in 2023, as Meta’s final supported VR device, at least until the company launches its next-level VR glasses next year.

Meta recently previewed the coming VR device, which impressed analysts at its Connect 2026 event.

The new device is lighter and more comfortable to use than previous iterations, and it also includes more advanced tech elements to power all-new VR experiences.

But it’ll be costly. Meta said that the device will cost $1299 at launch, which is more than double the current price of a Quest 3.

Given the general lack of consumer interest and engagement with VR, that seems like a big ask. Then again, if Meta invests more into VR development and the integration of its creative AI tools into the VR environment, that could spark a new wave of interest in the tech.

#7477. Google’s Search Volume Is About 5x ChatGPT’s, OpenAI Says via @sejournal, @MattGSouthern

See how Search Engine Journal checks a real site for crawler access and ranks fixes by business impact.

Invalid traffic on AI Max search doubled to 5.28%. Get the channel-by-channel retail benchmarks before Black Friday.

Invalid traffic on AI Max search doubled to 5.28%. Get the channel-by-channel retail benchmarks before Black Friday.

This rundown shows how to test briefs, audits, or rank reporting with a four-layer system built on your own data.

Ask the SEJ experts you already read what AI, agents, and algorithm changes mean for your traffic. Private, human to human. $97/mo.

Webinar: State Of Agentic AEO For 2027: What’s Scaling & What’s Stalling

ChatGPT's search-like queries equal about a fifth of Google's volume, its maker says, counting each question that sends it to the web. Traffic data differs.

ChatGPT receives roughly one-fifth as many search-like queries as does Google, according to ChatGPT consumer product lead Adam Fry.

The point he made about the volume of search-like queries was shared with Alex Kantrowitz in an open discussion during a Monday evening dinner in New York hosted by OpenAI. The transcript was later posted by Big Technology on Tuesday.

OpenAI counts a search each time a question sends ChatGPT to the web, Fry said. The visits ChatGPT sends to websites are a separate measure, and outside data on that traffic puts ChatGPT far behind Google.

Alex Kantrowitz of Big Technology asked Fry whether a conversation that includes a search counts as one, or whether each question requiring a web search counts separately. Fry replied:

“Every time you ask a question and go search the web, that’s a search.”

He said people first came to ChatGPT mostly for writing and coding help, which they wouldn’t have searched for. Over the past year, he said, they’ve brought questions about people, places and things that need up-to-date information, plus finance and health questions, where ChatGPT does a web search. He described the category as anything users would have taken to a traditional search engine.

Fry, in the published interview, did not reveal which Google figure was referenced; for how long the comparison is based on; nor does he state which regions are covered. He has also been asked if one-fifth of Google’s size makes ChatGPT multiple times bigger than Bing. The report by Big Technology indicates that the number of weekly active users of ChatGPT grew by 20% (from 1 billion to 1.2 billion) over the last two months.

In February, Ahrefs had estimated the search volume of ChatGPT to be around 12 percent that of Google’s search volume using a definition of search that was different from that used by OpenAI. It began with 2.5 billion daily ChatGPT prompts in July 2025 (from a study by Harvard University and OpenAI) and determined that approximately 65 percent were the type of query users would have previously looked up on Google.

Using this data, it compared those numbers with the over 5 trillion searches per year Google announced in March 2025. The author said that 65% was higher than the share the researchers had classified as search. That method sorts prompts by the kind of request, while Fry’s figure counts web searches.

Ahrefs also tracks where traffic to websites comes from across sites that use its free Web Analytics tool. In October, across 118,671 sites, Google accounted for 24.74% of tracked traffic and ChatGPT for 0.36%, according to the tracker’s Nov. 3 update.

Alphabet’s third-quarter release, which I covered on Oct. 28, gives Google’s search query growth as a rate rather than a count. In the release, CEO Sundar Pichai said AI investments are driving overall search query growth and that Google’s “year-over-year query growth rate is the highest it’s been in the last five years.”

OpenAI’s data comparing search-like queries by users of ChatGPT versus those using Google indicates the approximate 1/5th ratio. Fry’s counting rule includes only the search requests made by ChatGPT users in order to send a question to the Web. Ahrefs’ referral tracker tracks the number of visitors that are directed to websites. In this data set, Google has an overwhelming amount of referrals compared to ChatGPT.

Neither Fry’s number nor Google’s year-over-year growth rate illustrates if ChatGPT searches are replacing searches on Google. If ChatGPT’s size is included in a channel plan, then when reporting search it should be accompanied by the definitions of query volume and referral traffic, which will provide two different metrics.

Ahrefs’ tracker reports referral shares month by month, giving you a regular outside perspective on the traffic ChatGPT sends. OpenAI’s roughly one-fifth ratio is difficult to compare with other estimates until the company clarifies which Google figure it used.

Follow on YouTube. Matt G. Southern is the Senior News Writer at Search Engine Journal, where he’s covered Google, SEO, ...

#7476. LinkedIn shares information on new creator insights options

The company’s updated analytics are designed to help creators access more monetization opportunities and better understand post metrics.

LinkedIn shared some new insights into its latest performance analytics options, which will provide more insight into what’s working and what’s not within each user’s content approach.

Last month, as part of a broader update of its creator tools, LinkedIn announced an updated profile insights dashboard, with more data on content performance and monetization opportunities.

On Tuesday, LinkedIn Director of Product, Content Sam Corrao Clannon shared some additional insights into how the new analytics elements will function, and how they’ll help creators maximize in-app opportunities.

In terms of presentation, LinkedIn's added a new Post Performance Table, which displays post info and key performance metrics together in one listing.

As shown in this example image, the new table shows each post’s impressions, views and watch time stats in one view. Corrao Clannon said this will make it easier for users to compare key performance metrics “without needing to open the analytics for each one of your posts individually.”

Corrao Clannon also highlighted several updated metric elements:

Corrao Clannon said that LinkedIn will be assessing how these new metrics are being used by creators as the company assesses the next steps to improve creator experience.

LinkedIn is also developing new creator monetization options as it works to drive more engagement in the feed.

#7474. Staff Engineer (remote work) at CloudLinux

TuxCare offers a comprehensive portfolio of security solutions for Linux and open-source software designed for enterprise organizations.

With TuxCare, companies can automate vulnerability patching, minimize downtime, maintain security and compliance, and rely on deep expertise in Linux and open-source security.

Our solutions cover widely used Linux distributions, end-of-life systems, open-source software ecosystems, and other critical infrastructure.

We are currently hiring Staff Engineers for two TuxCare products: KernelCare and SecureChain.

KernelCare provides automated live patching for Linux kernels without requiring reboots, helping organizations keep production systems secure while avoiding operational downtime.

As a mature and business-critical product, KernelCare offers the opportunity to solve complex engineering challenges at scale, evolve established systems, and improve automation while maintaining a strong focus on reliability and stability.

Learn more at: https://tuxcare.com/enterprise-live-patching-services/kernelcare-enterprise/

SecureChain is one of TuxCare's newer security products focused on open-source software supply chain security.

SecureChain helps organizations use open-source dependencies more safely by providing packages rebuilt from verified source code, scanned for malware and supply chain risks, and continuously patched as new vulnerabilities are discovered.

As part of the SecureChain team, you will help build the next generation of automation and high-reliability systems behind these processes, evaluating and improving existing workflows and using AI where appropriate to analyze data, support decisions, and improve operational visibility.

Because SecureChain is still a relatively new product, engineers have significant opportunities to build systems from the ground up and influence its technical direction.

Learn more at: https://tuxcare.com/securechain-for-oss/

The role combines deep technical execution with technical product ownership and is approximately 70% hands-on software engineering and 30% technical product ownership.

You will lead significant product components and workstreams end-to-end: clarifying requirements with stakeholders, defining milestones, designing solutions, contributing production code, delivering functionality to customers, and ensuring its continued reliability in production.

This is an individual contributor role, not a people-management position. We are looking for someone who can operate autonomously, navigate ambiguity, and drive complex initiatives forward without relying on a manager to define every next step.

As the product and priorities evolve, curiosity, adaptability, and a broad interest in the cybersecurity area will be important for success in this role.

#7470. Reddit tests ads in search results

The platform wants to help advertisers align their promotions with users’ discovery behavior even as the company’s overall search traffic drops.

Reddit is testing ads in search results in the app, as the platform looks for more ways to help advertisers align with discovery behaviors.

As shown in these example screens, Reddit’s search ads will be targeted by query, and will include a small ad marker in the upper section of the panel.

As explained by Reddit: “People come to Reddit seeking human perspectives and experiences they can’t find elsewhere, with half of Reddit users saying the platform helps them discover something new. As we continue improving the user search experience on Reddit, we’re also beginning to test ad placements in search. These placements leverage existing ad targeting in search results pages on Reddit, which have high purchase and search intent.”

Reddit said the promotions will be shown in search results based on a range of factors, though these will primarily be aligned with contextual relevance.

Reddit has been working to improve its discovery tools as the app tries to help users find more relevant matches for their queries.

In July, Reddit published a study that found that 72% of its users come to the platform seeking information, and 64% of them have a specific goal in mind.

That also aligns with how Reddit gains traffic from search results, since the platform gets significant citation volume from AI chatbots.

However, AI traffic has started to dry up. Last week, as part of its Q3 performance update, Reddit CEO Steve Huffman said that the company’s search traffic dropped by more than 1 million referrals per week on average in October.

That could reduce the value of its own in-app discovery process, though Tuesday’s announcement suggests that Reddit is clearly hoping to maintain its value as a product discovery tool.

Reddit said that it will initially test ads in search with a select group of eligible advertisers.

#7468. Cloud Platform Engineer at Supabase

As a Cloud Platform Engineer at Supabase, you’ll join Cloud Engineering, part of Engineering Operations, and shape the foundations that let engineering teams build, ship, and operate infrastructure safely and independently across AWS and GCP.

This is a role spanning cloud governance, platform engineering, and hands-on operations. You’ll work across AWS and GCP account structure, identity and access management, Infrastructure as Code (IaC), reusable platform primitives, security boundaries, and self-service workflows for engineers.

You’ll be joining at a point where many of these foundations are being actively defined or reworked. We’re looking for someone who can take ambiguous, messy, business-critical infrastructure problems and turn them into pragmatic systems, tools, standards, and written guidance that scale with the company and empower teams to go faster.

Designing and implementing cloud governance across AWS and GCP, including identity, account organization, access controls, and guardrails.

Building reusable infrastructure and building blocks using Infrastructure as Code (IaC), enabling teams to provision and manage infrastructure safely and consistently.

Creating self-service capabilities that reduce organizational friction and let engineers move more quickly within sensible boundaries.

Owning resource governance and visibility: management tagging, a trustworthy inventory of what exists and who owns it, and consistent cross-account billing data.

Developing reusable security primitives, including secrets storage, KMS, certificate management, token vending, direct service-to-service authentication, mTLS, and related platform services.

Owning cloud-level network and operational primitives: IP address management, DNS, and health signals such as service-limit and quota alerting.

Combining software engineering with hands-on operations and total ownership: building, deploying, monitoring, troubleshooting, and improving what you own.

Establishing and sharing cloud engineering best practices through documentation, internal tooling, examples, and enablement.

Have 5+ years of experience in cloud governance, platform engineering, and/or operations, have operated production systems at scale, and carried on-call for them.

Have previously undertaken a significant migration, e.g. rearchitected an AWS account topology, introduced IaC for existing infrastructure, centralized identity/SSO, or brought a second cloud under governance.

Enjoy building internal platform and self-service experiences for other engineers.

Are genuinely deep in at least one—ideally two—of the three strength areas below (and tell us which is your center of gravity):

Tooling & ecosystem: You’re intimately familiar with the breadth of AWS services in general, and IAM/SSO in particular. You know their Well-Architected Framework, and when to reach outside. You have working knowledge of GCP. You use IaC routinely and have run it in production, whichever tool. You’ve probably collaborated with auditors on meeting compliance requirements.

Software engineering: You write real production code (Go, Rust, or a comparable systems language), and can build reliable, maintainable services. You thrive across all stages of the SDLC, set technical direction, know how to de-risk projects, and produce written artifacts such as RFCs.

Operations: You’ve run production infrastructure yourself: carried the pager, written the alerts and runbooks, used SLOs or similar to decide what to fix first, and made shared systems less risky over time. You like owning systems, simplifying complex infrastructure problems, and turning best practices into reusable building blocks.

Are comfortable treating infrastructure as software and have relevant experience with IaC and automation.

Communicate clearly, work with a wide range of stakeholders, and thrive in async, globally distributed teams.

Are comfortable navigating ambiguity and iterating toward better systems over time.

We hire globally. We believe you can do your best work from anywhere. There are no Supabase offices, but we provide a WeWork membership or co-working allowance you can use anywhere in the world.

Every team member receives ESOP (equity ownership) in the company. We want everyone to share in the upside of what we’re building together.

Use this budget to set up your ideal work environment—laptop, monitor, headphones, or whatever helps you do your best work.

Supabase covers 100% of health insurance for employees and 80% for dependents, wherever you are. Your wellbeing and your family’s health are important to us.

Once a year, the entire company gathers in a new city for a week of connection, collaboration, and fun. It’s a highlight of our year.

We operate asynchronously and trust you to manage your own time. You know what needs to be done and when.

Every team member receives an annual education allowance to spend on learning—courses, books, conferences, or anything that supports your growth.

Supabase was born-remote and open-source-first. We believe our globally distributed team is our secret weapon in building tools developers love.

Over $1B raised (including our $500M Series F)

We move fast, build in public, and use what we ship. If it’s in your project, we probably use it in ours too. We believe deeply in the open-source ecosystem and strive to support—not replace—existing tools and communities.

#7467. Why JCPenney is taking on deal fatigue for its holiday campaign

Let Marketing Dive's free newsletter keep you informed, straight from your inbox.

Marisa Thalberg, chief customer and marketing officer at parent Catalyst Brands, explains the retailer’s focus on real people and no-fuss ways to save.

JCPenney is attempting to alleviate the deal fatigue felt by consumers headed into the holiday season with a new campaign focused on value and no-fuss ways to save, according to details shared with Marketing Dive.

The retailer’s holiday campaign launches Monday across TV, online video, audio and paid social and spotlights real deal-scoring JCPenney shoppers. The brand will also help consumers navigate the season with a weekly deals cheat sheet, in-store activations and the return of its “Really Big Deals” promotions.

A 15-second spot, titled “Party Fits,” is set in Crown Point, Indiana, and stars a local barber named Raemonie, who showcases three holiday looks that she purchased for a total of $168. In the ad, a voice-over says, “Meet Raemonie — she was worried about having enough outfits for all of her holiday parties. Now, thanks to JCPenney, she’s worried about having enough holiday parties for all her outfits.”

The campaign, which builds on a “Yes, JCPenney” brand platform launched in 2025, was created with agency Mischief and follows a back-to-school effort that tapped real teachers for the creative.

“While everyone else is doing kind of their fantastical TV campaigns for holiday, ours is truly grounded in real people, real life, and showing how those real people can — and deserve to — enjoy having better deals from JCPenney,” said Marisa Thalberg, executive vice president and chief customer and marketing officer at parent Catalyst Brands.

JCPenney’s latest campaign taps into the insight that consumers are overwhelmed with deals as brands attempt to break through amid a period marked by economic uncertainty. A barrage of deals may also be leaving consumers feeling skeptical about whether or not they are actually saving any money at all. Only 32% of shoppers report that they completely or mostly trust retailers to offer a fair or competitive price, according to Akeneo data cited in press details.

On the flip side, 92% of consumers say they are confident that they receive good deals at JCPenney, per information shared by the brand. To help consumers navigate its various promotions, JCPenney will release a cheat sheet each Monday throughout the season highlighting the top deals to shop each week. The “Really Big Deals” promotional offer will run every Thursday through Dec. 13.

From Nov. 5-11, 97% of JCPenney’s in-store items will be on sale. Additionally, the retailer is offering Black Friday deals from Nov. 20-29, and surprise giveaways in stores on Nov. 27 at various points throughout the day.

In hopes of attracting additional foot traffic, JCPenney teamed with Warner Bros. Global Consumer Products around Harry Potter for pop-up events and to bring a version of the Hogwarts Express train to select stores. A limited-time Harry Potter capsule offers various clothing and home items.

Consumers are expected to spend an average of $1,654 this season, a 4% increase over the year prior, as they anticipate higher prices, according to Deloitte’s annual holiday survey. While 38% percent of shoppers plan to adopt five or more money-saving strategies, that share of consumers is also expected to spend 12% more than others on average.

The holiday season will be critical for JCPenney amid ongoing sales struggles. The company saw total net sales fall over 8% year over year to $1.3 billion in the second quarter, a weaker performance than others in the department store segment, including Kohl’s and Macy’s.

Deals have been a staple of JCPenney’s marketing, both around the holidays and beyond. Earlier this year, the retailer remade Paris Fashion Week with a runway show that swapped haute couture for JCPenney looks, with prices displayed on stage. In August, the brand parodied off-price retailers as a way to tout the quality it offers despite its low prices.

With its latest move, JCPenney hopes to continue reinforcing for its shoppers that they don’t have to choose between fashionable items and affordability, Thalberg explained.

“We understand our customer,” Thalberg said. “Our customer is everyone, real people, real working middle class American families who are trying to make ends meet, and we are very cognizant and empathetic of the economic pressures that Americans are facing.”

#7463. Amazon says more than 400M customers have used Alexa for Shopping in the last 12 months

Join us Dec. 1-3 in Nashville for the Digiday Programmatic Marketing Summit

This story was first published by Digiday sibling Modern Retail.

During its fiscal third-quarter earnings call on Thursday, Amazon shed more light on how its conversational AI shopping assistant, Alexa for Shopping, is powering more transactions.

More than 400 million customers have now used Alexa for Shopping in the last 12 months, up from 350 million at the end of July. Meanwhile, customers who use Alexa for Shopping are spending 50% more than those who don’t, Amazon CEO Andy Jassy said during the company’s earnings call on last Thursday.

The figures represent how AI shopping has become a powerful growth lever for Amazon and a way to nudge shoppers toward more sales, especially in a time when the business has matured and growth in other areas is harder to come by.

On Thursday, Amazon reported that net sales for the third quarter came in at $201.9 billion, up 12% from last year. The bulk of that came from Amazon Web Services, or AWS, which reported segment sales up 39% year over year.

Growth in many of Amazon’s e-commerce-related segments slowed during the quarter. However, much of that was because Prime Day was held at the end of June this year, rather than in mid-July. As a result, Amazon recorded the sales growth from Prime Day in the second quarter; last year, it was recorded in the third quarter.

Online Stores revenue was up 2% year over year. Last year, during the third quarter, Amazon reported that online Stores revenue grew 10% between the third quarter of 2024 and 2025.

Similarly, third-party seller services and advertising services grew 12% and 24%, respectively, between the third quarter of 2024 and the third quarter of 2025. By contrast, between the third quarter of 2025 and the third quarter of 2026, they grew 9% and 11%, respectively.

The public-facing history of Alexa for Shopping goes back three years. In February 2024, Amazon released an AI shopping assistant that was then known as Rufus. In May of this year, Amazon rebranded Rufus to Alexa for Shopping, choosing to adopt the name of its conversational voice assistant many shoppers were already used to.

Since then, Amazon has made Alexa for Shopping more visible on its website and app, and added new capabilities.

Amazon has made headlines for its walled-garden approach to AI, as it has blocked platforms like ChatGPT and Meta’s Muse shopping agent from scraping its site.

Jassy hinted during Thursday’s earnings call that that may eventually change — which he has said before — but ultimately, he said, the company wants to push as many customers to Alexa for Shopping as possible.

“Though we anticipate working with horizontal agents, we believe Alexa for Shopping is going to be the primary shopping agent customers use to shop Amazon,” Jassy said.

Google search was the line item buyers never questioned. That’s changing.

AI agents are becoming common among agencies, but most companies haven’t grasped their potential to reshape teams.

The brands showing up at the annual race represent a more diverse cross-section of retail than ever before.

#7461. Your buyers are sending agents. Will they recommend your brand?

For most of my career, marketing and sales have run on a single assumption: a person is paying attention. We wrote for human eyes, optimized for human clicks, and designed every interaction for human discernment. That assumption is starting to break.

Buyers are sending AI agents out ahead of them that have been tasked to read what you publish, compare you against the field, and hand the user a shortlist, often before that user lands on your site or talks to sales. At the same time, marketing and sales teams are building their own AI systems to keep pace. Both shifts are landing at once, and together they change how demand gets created and how trust gets earned.

This is the biggest shift in marketing since search itself. The brands that come out ahead will be the ones that get their foundation right first, because there is no AI strategy without a data strategy.

We have optimized for machines before; that was the whole idea behind search. What is happening now is a different order of magnitude. Agents are constantly reading, comparing, and recommending next steps based on users’ prompts.

That changes my job and yours. An agent now reads our story, determines whether our brand looks clear and trustworthy, and uses that read to decide whether we make the shortlist it hands to a decision-maker. Clear, consistent, well-structured content is what tips that decision and most brands will never know when they have been left off the list.

Enterprise marketing has always been episodic. We run a campaign, pass a lead, let sales run a play, and start over. Agent-driven buyers no longer move on that calendar, so our engines must evolve.

The shift now is toward an always-on system that reads each customer continuously, puts the best opportunities first, and evolves in the moment. This new process is rewiring how marketing and sales work together, from a handoff between two teams into one operation looking at the same customer at the same time.

But none of it works without a trustworthy foundation. If revenue or pipeline means six different things in six systems, layering AI on top will only scale the confusion. Governed, well-understood data is what lets an always-on engine move fast without getting reckless. When everyone has access to the same models, data becomes your differentiator because customer history and context are yours alone.

There is of course a grim version of this future as well. Infinite personalization collapses into infinite noise and every inbox fills with confident, hollow outreach. I think that outcome is a choice, not an inevitability.

Used well, AI takes the mechanical work off our teams: the assembling, formatting, and first draft nobody enjoys. When it gives back time, that time is only worth something if people spend it where machines cannot: understanding a customer, building a real relationship, making the call on what comes next.

That does not happen by accident. It takes leaders willing to rewrite roles, retrain teams, and give people room to experiment. But the judgment, creativity, and taste required to plan and execute differentiated marketing at scale will always remain human.

Your next customer may arrive as an agent or send one ahead. When that agent builds its shortlist, I want Snowflake on it, and I want your company on it too. That is the work in front of every marketing and sales leader now.

#7458. Senior Product Operations Manager at LiveKit

LiveKit is building the infrastructure layer for the agentic era of computing. Our platform gives developers everything they need to build, test, deploy, scale, and observe AI agents in production. Founded in 2021, LiveKit powers voice and agentic AI applications for tens of thousands of developers, collectively facilitating billions of calls each year: language tutors that talk back, customer support and sales agents running real call volume, healthcare intake, coaching and training software, in-game characters, and the voice and video layer for robots.

We're looking for our first Product Operations Manager. Our product team is small, senior, and growing fast, with each PM coming in to establish the foundations of a new part of the business. As the team grows, the work that connects it (how we plan, set and measure goals, keep the roadmap honest, run our rituals, learn from customers, and bring new people up to speed) needs an owner.

This role is that owner. Your customers are the PMs and the head of product, and your job is to make them faster and better at what they do. You won't be inheriting a process manual or enforcing one. You'll be designing how a product team works at the moment it goes from a handful of people to a real organization, inside an engineering culture that values autonomy and only adopts what makes people's lives easier.

see the people you support as customers, and start from their problems rather than from a framework

like building systems from scratch more than maintaining them

would rather remove a meeting than add one

write clearly and enjoy making complicated things legible

are a fast learner, frequently picking up new tools and techniques, including AI

The best way to impress us is by trying LiveKit's product, understanding how we work, and bringing smart thoughts and opinions to the table.

Run the product team's operating cadence. You own the calendar of planning cycles, reviews, and recurring updates: what each one is for, who needs to be there, what goes in, and what comes out. You make sure decisions and follow-ups are captured and visible to everyone who needs them, and you keep retiring or reshaping rituals that stop earning their time.

Own the roadmap as a system. You own how the roadmap is structured, at the right altitude for each audience, from engineering teams to leadership to sales. You make sure it stays current and becomes the place people go to find out what we're building and where it stands.

Make goals and progress visible. You run goal-setting for the product team so that every goal is measurable against a clear source of truth, and you track progress against those goals through the quarter. With our data team, you own the reporting the product team and leadership rely on, and you flag drift early.

Keep the product development playbook. You maintain lightweight shared standards for each step from framing a problem through launch: problem briefs, launch readiness, product stage definitions, release notes. With marketing, you run the internal side of every launch so each one ships with docs, pricing, and GTM ready.

Run the customer insight engine. You own the systems that keep customers close to the product team: a research repository people use, a continuous pipeline of customer conversations set up with sales and success, and a steady synthesis of what we hear from sales, support, surveys, and deals, delivered to the PMs who need it.

Grow the craft of the PM team. You own onboarding for new PMs, ongoing training, and the team's community of practice, and you help shape how we hire and level PMs.

Choose and run the team's tools. You decide how the product team uses its tools, from roadmapping and analytics to AI, and you make sure PMs get real leverage from them.

Be a partner in running the product org. You keep a pulse on where the team is stuck, bring your own point of view on what to fix next, and work with the head of product to make the product org better every quarter.

6+ years of experience, with at least 3 in product operations or a closely related role (chief of staff to a product leader, product program management, product strategy and operations) supporting a product team.

You've built it before. You've designed a product team's operating cadence from scratch at a company that was scaling, not only kept an existing one running.

A force multiplier. You measure your success by what changed for the people you support, not by how much process exists.

A strong writer. Most of what this role produces is read by other people: templates, updates, plans, and documentation.

Data fluent. You're comfortable defining metrics, working in BI tools, and telling a trustworthy number from an untrustworthy one. SQL is a plus.

Technical enough for a developer company. Our customers are developers and our organization is engineering-led. You don't need to write code, but you need to be credible with the people who do.

Influence without authority. Our engineering teams operate with high autonomy. Nothing here works by mandate; it works because it's useful.

AI-native. You use AI tools heavily in your own work and can help others do the same.

Product operations experience at a developer tools, API, infrastructure, or AI company

Ran quarterly planning or OKRs for a product organization

Built a research repository or a customer recruiting program for B2B research

Built a PM onboarding program or career ladder

Worked with go-to-market teams on launch readiness and product stage definitions

An opportunity to build something truly impactful to the world

Contribute to open source alongside world-class engineers

#7457. Google search loses its free pass with buyers

Join us Dec. 1-3 in Nashville for the Digiday Programmatic Marketing Summit

For the better part of two decades, Google search was the line item media buyers rarely had to defend. AI Overviews complicated that. Google now answers more queries on the results page itself, which changes how often people click through to an advertiser and what each click is worth.

That, in turn, means fewer prime ad slots around the search results people see, and Google’s automated buying tools, now the default for most campaigns, are quick to pay up for the ones that remain. Q4 makes it worse as seasonal advertisers bid up CPCs in the quarter that matters most.

Nearly a month into that quarter, advertisers want to know what all that extra spend is buying. Across conversations with 18 media buyers, Digiday found clients putting Google through the kind of scrutiny usually saved for newer channels.

“We’re having to look much more closely at what happens after the click and whether those clicks are actually producing qualified leads and revenue,” said Lindsay Marty, founder of Above the Bar Marketing.

That’s left her clients increasingly open to shifting a portion of their Google search budgets into channels including Meta and Local Services Ads if performance weakens. Marty added: “The amount being questioned is generally around 10% to 20% of the Google budget when that conversation comes up.”

Google’s own numbers point the same way. Search ad revenue grew 12% in the third quarter, Alphabet’s slowest pace this year, and executives blamed part of the slowdown on AI Mode — Google’s chatbot-style search tab.

The numbers are part of a bigger shift in how people use the internet. More of them are handing the search, and sometimes the purchase itself, to AI agents, and the money underneath the web is starting to follow. Where it lands is anyone’s guess. Advertisers and their agencies are having to place their bets now.

Take Bryce Hamlin, vp of data intelligence at Power Digital. He said media mix modelling and geo-testing were showing a greater impact from upper-funnel media and less from search. Dollars tend to follow whatever the models credit.

“Google search ads are still very powerful, but in the next two-to-three years, we will see a lot of searches happen off of Google because of LLMs,” he said. “We’re already seeing the beginning of this trend.”

Hamlin said the growing use of marketing science, AI and incremental measurement is also prompting advertisers to question whether some of their Google search budget would work harder elsewhere.

“An increasing number of marketers are asking, ‘is a portion of my budget better spent elsewhere’ — to the tune of 10% to 30% of the Google search budget getting reallocated,” he added.

Accuracast’s group CEO Farhad Divecha said the percentage within the Google budgets being questioned by his team’s clients is about 10% for now, but given the dynamic and competitive nature of Black Friday and  Cyber Monday, this could change to as much as 20% to 30% quite rapidly.

The size of those budgets vary, though search remains one of the biggest lines in most media plans. It took $114.2 billion of U.S. digital ad spending last year, or 38.8% of the total, according to the Interactive Advertising Bureau and PricewaterhouseCoopers. For mid-sized and larger advertisers that employ agencies, a Google search budget can run from tens of thousands of dollars a month into the millions.

Branded keyword CPCs rose 5% in the third quarter, roughly in line with non-branded terms, according to Michelle Merklin, vp of search at Tinuiti.

“CPCs keep rising,” she continued, though she’s keeping a closer eye on how much search there is to buy. Impressions and clicks climbed over the same period, which leaves advertisers paying a bit more for a bigger pool of demand.

Some buyers have done the math on that trade and don’t like the answer.

“It’s not just traffic, but also the ROI from Google that’s been squeezed in recent years, making it less attractive as a channel,” said Divecha. “We definitely see advertisers looking at other options to invest budgets in, especially ChatGPT.”

He said Accuracast’s clients are looking at alternatives and could move money away from Google if click volumes began to dip. Some execs are finding that channels outside search play a greater role in driving conversions than traditional attribution models suggest. The agency’s AI-led campaign management platform, Unyte.AI, for example, automatically reallocates budget away from Google when returns weaken, shifting spend to other platforms delivering incremental conversions at a better return on investment, Divecha explained.

More of that money is landing on Meta and TikTok, where advertisers are trying to build awareness and consideration earlier in the funnel. According to Merklin, some advertisers were reallocating budgets from less efficient parts of search, including campaigns targeting competitors’ keywords or “conquesting.” But she said most advertisers managed by Tinuiti were seeking incremental budget or reallocating spend from elsewhere, rather than cutting search to fund upper-funnel activity; the trend contributed to a 16% rise in Facebook ad spend among Tinuiti’s U.S. clients in the third quarter.

“You have to create the demand before it can be captured,” she explained. “There’s been a shift recently into the mid and upper funnel in order to produce more brand search volume.”

That shift doesn’t necessarily mean advertisers are abandoning search altogether. Some see paid search as increasingly important as organic traffic comes under pressure.

“It’s only [becoming] more important to be in that paid space because organic search traffic is being impacted,” said Casey Sheehan, vp, group director at RPA.

That said, fewer buyers treat it as the bedrock of a media plan.

“What’s coming up more in client conversations is the need to be present earlier in the discovery journey,” said Mark Byrne, director of paid media at Brave Bison. “Google search remains strong at capturing existing demand, while paid social and YouTube help build awareness and consideration before someone actively searches.”

It’s a rethink that reaches all the way down to the search results page, where paid and organic listings now get judged together. Byrne said advertisers were looking more closely at where they needed to bid to capture valuable traffic, and where they could pull back, weighing the combined impact on revenue, return on ad spend and cost per acquisition.

“From our perspective the most effective strategy is to have a really strong presence in your organic AI citations, and then also a strong paid search presence,” said Wpromote’s head of paid search Valerie Dorer.

On that basis, what’s happening to search ad spending is arguably to be expected as Google’s ads business adjusts around however people are going to access and share information moving forward. Indeed, ads in AI Mode are already expanding to new markets in Brazil, Mexico, and the United Kingdom while it is also scaling the ads already present in markets like Australia, Canada, India and Kenya.

Victor Batista, global search lead at Jellyfish, put a finer point on it. He said clients were not reducing their overall Google spend “in a targeted manner”. Instead, the “Google ecosystem is expanding its footprint in discussions as the AI experience is enhanced and more widely understood,” he added.

Clients are, in other words, identifying opportunities to shift spending from traditional keyword-based search campaigns to AI-powered alternatives such as AI Max and Performance Max, while also increasing investment in YouTube and Demand Gen. Overall, Batista said, budget diversification remained “generally contained within the Google ecosystem.”

Google remains a major channel for capturing existing demand, particularly when consumers are actively searching for products or services. But as search behavior changes and advertisers take a closer look at which channels are driving incremental returns, Google is facing greater scrutiny over its place in clients’ media plans.

“The incremental 10% to 20% is becoming less automatic,” said Marty. “Advertisers increasingly want Google to prove that spend is still the best use of those dollars.”

In response to this article, a Google spokesperson said: “Every day, millions of businesses choose Search Ads to drive growth. AI Overviews create new opportunities for brands to be discovered and help shoppers make more confident decisions. As Search becomes more conversational, our AI-powered campaigns help advertisers capture these new opportunities, while keeping them firmly in control.”

Customers who use Alexa for Shopping are spending 50% more than those who don’t.

AI agents are becoming common among agencies, but most companies haven’t grasped their potential to reshape teams.

The brands showing up at the annual race represent a more diverse cross-section of retail than ever before.

#7455. Express unwraps holiday campaign to build on brand metric gains

Let Marketing Dive's free newsletter keep you informed, straight from your inbox.

CMO Joe Berean explains how “Holiday, Hamlin Style” was inspired by the organic social engagement of the retailer’s spring campaign.

Express has enlisted a family of pop cultural figures for its holiday campaign, according to details shared with Marketing Dive. The “Holiday, Hamlin Style” effort reimagines holiday traditions and unites actor-model-reality TV star Lisa Rinna; her husband, actor Harry Hamlin; her daughter, model Delilah Belle Hamlin; and Delilah’s boyfriend, “Love Island” veteran Mick Maio.

Rinna, who began her career as a model and on soap operas before becoming a breakout star on “The Real Housewives of Beverly Hills,” has a long-standing place in pop culture and connects with different consumer groups in different ways. She also has displayed a gift for virality, whether on reality TV or competition shows including “Dancing with the Stars” and “The Traitors.”

“That connection that she has to people just really comes through so authentically and translates so well into today's social media era. That's exactly what we captured on the campaign, too,” Express CMO Joe Berean said. “We designed it as one that can have that polish of a traditional holiday campaign, but really be social-first.”

The social-first push is in line with Express’ other marketing efforts, like the Expressionists, its community of creators, associates and brand ambassadors that is helping to bring to life “Dress to Express,” a brand platform centered around instilling confidence and inspiring self-expression.

Express in August launched a fall campaign, “Too Easy to Look This Good,” that featured NFL player Tee Higgins and pop star Tinashe, weeks before her album “Popstar” took the charts and the pop culture world by storm. The campaign generated the brand’s highest earned media and highest paid media traffic to its website for the year so far. From Q2 into Q3, Express saw brand awareness increase 3% and consideration increase 2%, Berean said.

“We're looking to move the needle in terms of bringing our brand back, reigniting the heat of the brand at its heyday and even exceeding that,” Berean said. “We've seen this sequential improvement of awareness and consideration, which is exactly the intent, especially as we're going into this crowded holiday season.”

Express has been looking to reheat its brand since filing for Chapter 11 in April 2024. The retailer and its Bonobos subsidiary are owned by Phoenix Retail, a joint venture from Simon Property Group, Brookfield Properties, Centennial and brand management firm WHP Global.

The campaign is Express’ latest effort that seeks to build buzz for the brand with celebrity-led activations, and arrives as the retailer works to engage both its existing customer base and a new generation of shoppers.

“The intent of the holiday campaign is to break through during a very crowded holiday season, make a splash and have it be scroll-stopping with Lisa's one-liners and dance moves and this really loving, authentic and stylish family dynamic that only they can bring,” Berean said.

“Holiday, Hamlin Style” follows a successful spring campaign that also featured Belle Hamlin and Maio. “It Couple, It Looks” launched in April and delivered the brand’s highest organic social engagement of the year so far, the retailer reported. In addition, Express said it has seen success with the multigenerational marketing approach in a Mother’s Day campaign starring model Karolína Kurková and her daughter.

“We've been trying different tactics to reach new customers… and those customers span generations,” Berean said of the holiday campaign. “To have a total family dynamic with mother, daughter, father and boyfriend was strategic, in that sense, to appeal to a wide audience.”

Shot in Los Angeles, the holiday campaign includes a series of seasonal vignettes and is set to remixes of Tchaikovsky’s “The Nutcracker.” It will run across video, social, digital and in-store channels throughout the season, highlighting styles priced from $38 to $298. The campaign arrives as big box retailers Target and Walmart and retail brands including Anthropologie and Sephora launch holiday campaigns.

“The competition is starting earlier. It's getting more aggressive. So having talent and campaigns that can really connect with people and break through is the name of the game,” Berean said.

#7452. Giggly Squad’s SiriusXM deal signals a shift from podcast networks to creator ecosystems

Join us Dec. 1-3 in Nashville for the Digiday Programmatic Marketing Summit

Last month, SiriusXM signed a multi-year deal with creators Paige DeSorbo and Hannah Berner for their Giggly Squad podcast reportedly valued at $160 million. Weeks later, the duo sold out four different nights for a live podcast tour at Madison Square Garden. That unprecedented deal signals a broader shift in podcasting: platforms are competing for creator-led businesses, not just shows.

“It’s even caught us on our heels, honestly, the demand for Giggly,” said Kelli Hurley, vp and head of content and podcast partnership at SiriusXM. “We were prepared, we were all hands on deck, but it has been just unprecedented in terms of what we’re seeing from the marketplace.”

Tom Webster, partner at trade association Sounds Profitable, said that despite podcast reach remaining flat since 2024, 8% of people who have consumed a podcast say they started in just the last 30 days. That’s a steady influx of new customers, and platforms like Spotify, Apple, and SiriusXM are trying to court them.

SiriusXM svp of podcast content Adam Sachs started working in podcasting 15 years ago, and he’s had a bird’s-eye view of the shifting landscape.

“The power of the network was what mattered [back then]. We would have these horizontal networks with a bunch of different shows in the network that all shared a sensibility,” he explained. “Now the approach is way more verticalized. We’re working with specific talent as opposed to a specific network, and helping them build a vertical ecosystem where their fans can engage. It’s sort of flipped.”

Building those vertical ecosystems can involve simple, expected things like helping a creator with their AV setup to far-reaching, off-platform support that includes IRL events.

“When we’re making these deals, we are increasingly thinking about a podcast as audio, video, social, and live. It’s those four legs of the stool, and we want to be a part of all of it,” said Sachs.

SiriusXM isn’t the only platform looking to work with creators beyond just hosting their show. Bryan Barletta, partner at Sounds Profitable and president of Podcast Movement, said Amazon’s investment in the NFL brother duo podcast, New Heights, signals a major shift in the partnership dynamic.

“We’re entering a new phase of podcasting where a distinct upper tier of creators is emerging, with significant separation from those coming up behind them,” he said.

The top tier of podcasting is drawing live audiences at arena scale. Giggly Squad is the first female-hosted podcast to headline Madison Square Garden, and tickets that averaged in the mid-$100s sold out fast. The sheer scope of the Giggly deal led Sirius to take a step back, according to Hurley. “In the past couple of weeks we took a total pause…we’ve got to do a full reset in how we want to go to market with this asset,” she said.

And brands are increasingly looking to get involved at multiple points in a creator podcast’s presence, from branded equipment to distribution support to showing up at IRL events. Now, more brands are heading into partnership talks wanting to “buy a creator,” according to Hurley. They’re asking for exclusivity, but Sirius wants to give them more.

“[We want] to be a one-stop shop,” she said.

Other platforms are offering a variety of ways for creators to connect with brands. Substack recently launched early access signups for dynamic ad insertion and plans for creators to leverage programmatic sponsorships in early 2027. Beehiiv just launched similar tools, dropping dynamic ad insertion, campaign management, and programmatic access just this past week.

“It gives creators more flexibility: run their own sponsor campaigns, put current ads into older episodes, and open unsold spots to programmatic demand when there’s a match,” said Ainsley Rossitto, head of podcast at beehiiv.

Since the mid-2020s, podcasts have aggressively pivoted to video. Sounds Profitable reports that now, only 17% of Americans expect a podcast to be audio-only. Substack’s head of video, Zach Taylor, told Digiday that half of their 350 highest revenue creators were using both audio in video in April 2024. “By February 2025, that number had grown to 82%,” he said.

The inclusion of a video component for podcasts makes it easier for discovery, as show clips can circulate on other social media platforms like YouTube, TikTok, and Instagram.

“Your podcast doesn’t have to be on video, but you have to have video,” said Barletta. Sounds Profitable’s research showed 84% of podcast clip consumers say clips led them to become regular podcast listeners at least sometimes.

Sachs said SiriusXM is seeing algorithmic growth through YouTube because of its built-in discoverability.

There are new pivots on the horizon: short-form content and a return to traditional radio. Sachs said Sirius is looking to do more with creators who are short-form-only content (monetizing their content, empowering them to do live events, and maybe even getting them on the radio), and are also supporting podcasters like Giggly Squad in creating their own radio shows — all in service of grabbing bigger slices of that audience pie.

“The radio audience is very distinct from the podcast audience; it’s a really disparate audience in terms of demographics, so we don’t see that as cannibalization at all. We see it as incremental reach,” Sachs said. “Giggly Squad is going to have a significant radio component, as well.”

Publishers are pushing back against Meta’s AI scraping terms, testing their leverage over a platform they still rely on.

Political and news creators are relied on now more than ever, but what platforms are working for them? Which ones aren’t?

#7451. What Reddit’s Q3 earnings mean for marketers amid major traffic shift

Let Marketing Dive's free newsletter keep you informed, straight from your inbox.

The platform’s ad revenue climbed 54% YoY, but signs of a slowdown persist.

Despite ad revenue growing 54% year-over-year, Reddit’s stock took a nosedive after the closing bell following the release of its Q3 earnings Thursday, largely due to slowed user growth and plunging referral traffic. Revenue overall grew 53% to $895 million YoY.

The social media platform, which went public in 2024, has been riding a wave of explosive growth driven by increased advertising capabilities and its high citation rate by artificial intelligence. However, as growth shows signs of tapering off and daily active users remain flat quarter-over-quarter, Reddit’s future is less clear.

Below, Marketing Dive pulled together three numbers from Reddit’s most recent earnings materials that marketers should be aware of as the platform becomes a bigger player in the online advertising space.

Reddit’s ad revenue growth outpaced overall revenue growth by a point. While strong, ad revenue made bigger gains earlier in the year and grew 64% YoY in Q2 and 74% YoY in Q1.

Click volume grew more than 50% in the most recent quarter and conversion volume more than tripled YoY, said Jen Wong, Reddit’s chief operating officer, during an earnings call. The platform forged deeper relationships with existing advertisers, with average spending and new advertiser acquisition both on the upswing, Wong reported. There was also a more than 50% YoY growth in mid-market advertisers in Europe, the Middle East and Africa, as well as North America.

As shoppers are increasingly bombarded by synthetic content, Reddit remains a go-to for those looking for a human opinion and half of U.S. shoppers verify AI recommendations on the platform, according to Wong. This makes Reddit a prime environment for advertisers looking to go after high-intent audiences.

“We see significant opportunity ahead for our business, and we're making progress reflected in strong customer retention, growing share of wallet and more always-on advertiser relationships. These trends give us confidence in Reddit's ability to deliver durable, differentiated growth,” said Wong, during the call.

The average number of referrals dropped per week in October

The number of U.S. daily users fell from 53.2 million in Q2 to 50.7 million in Q3 while international users rose from 77.1 million to 79.6 million, according to a shareholder letter.

This falloff can be, in-part, attributed to a sharp decrease in referral traffic. In October, referrals are falling by over one million per week on average. If the trend continues, Reddit’s daily active unique user count could fall by low double-digits next quarter, according to the letter. Changes to how consumers use AI overviews on Google, which continue to routinely cite Reddit, are in no small part behind the drop, according to Steven Huffman, co-founder and CEO of Reddit, during an earnings call.

“The reality is the AI Mode does not drive traffic, not for us and not to anybody as far as I know. So it's just a fundamental shift in how Google works,” said Huffman during the call. “It used to be a traffic driver. Now it's not.”

The platform has existing relationships with AI developers. Just before it went public in 2024, the platform signed a $60 million-a-year AI content deal with Google. A few months later, it entered into a similar deal with OpenAI, the company behind ChatGPT. Both deals are set to expire in 2027. Google is still “crawling” the platform “as hard as ever,” per Huffman. He also maintains that Reddit is one of the most Googled terms, meaning demand for the platform remains high.

To help combat user loss, the platform is shifting to a “logged-in only” experience, Huffman announced during the call. Logged-in users make up the bulk of Reddit’s engagement and are more valuable to advertisers, according to the co-founder. It will also help to protect the platform’s data.

The QoQ revenue growth of Reddit’s Ads API ecosystem

Reddit continues to build out its advertising tools. During the quarter, Reddit Max, an automated campaign offering, entered public beta, according to Wong. Automation is one area of focus for the company, along with expanding Reddit for Business and delivering increased value to advertisers.

Reddit is also seeing strength in video, with video ad revenue growth more than doubling YoY in Q3, according to the shareholder letter. During the quarter, the platform launched a 15-second engaged video view, building on the momentum of a six-second option. Retailer JCPenney saw a 4-point lift in ad awareness and a 90% higher completion rate with the longer videos compared to the shorter ones, per the letter.

#7449. People Inc. is building custom APIs to make its journalism easier for AI agents to use

Join us Dec. 1-3 in Nashville for the Digiday Programmatic Marketing Summit

People Inc. is moving toward custom APIs to help AI agents access its content more efficiently.

Rather than making AI systems shift through full webpages, People Inc., is giving them the article text and key details in a simpler format, according to Jon Roberts, People Inc.’s chief innovation officer.

“If you are an agent and you’re reading an HTML page, 90% of the content is junk meant for a browser to read, not an agent,” Roberts told delegates at Digiday’s Media Strategies London event. “It will really distract the agent, and it will be incredibly expensive to process all these tokens that it doesn’t need.”

The custom APIs strip out that extra material, sending the article text in Markdown along with details such as who wrote it and when it was published or updated and other “trust signals that matter.” The move comes as People Inc. shifts its broader investment priorities: the company laid off about 6% of its workforce last week. The cuts underscore the pressure on publishers to find durable businesses as AI reshapes how audiences discover and access journalism.

People Inc. first gave partners access to its content through web crawling, then through Amazon S3 buckets, which let the company send updates and retract content. It is now moving toward custom APIs, Roberts said, because crawling is an inefficient way for AI systems to get information.

Strategically, that could make access more efficient and give People Inc. greater control over how its journalism is delivered to AI partners.

“Every token that needs to be processed is expensive,” said Roberts. “If you throw it the entire HTML header, that’s abuse. Right, that’s not reasonable.”

AI companies are beginning to recognize the importance of knowing when publishers created or updated content, Roberts said. People Inc. is building those details into its content for its own AI tools, then using what it learns to offer AI partners a more useful way to access that material. The publisher is not focused on advertising directly to AI agents, he added.

The urgency comes as traffic from AI agents and agentic browsers grew 7,851% year over year, according to HUMAN’s 2026 State of AI Traffic report.

The challenge is already pressing for publishers trying to distinguish useful, customer-led agents from bots scraping content. “We need to act quickly on it because it’s not going to be something in the future — it’s happening now,” Daily Mail director of SEO and editorial commerce Carly Steven said at Digiday’s Media Strategies London event. “So that is a fairly urgent need that we all need to address and get our heads around,” she added.

Publishers are testing different ways to adapt their content and services for AI systems. People Inc. is moving toward structured access for AI partners. The Economist is experimenting with making marketing and B2B sales material outside of its paywall more easily readable for agents. Time has also converted its pages from HTML to Markdown and has even started to sell ads to those agents visiting its site.

Alongside figuring out which automated requests to allow, publishers are experimenting with how to package their journalism for AI systems. Forbes is also testing how to make some content more usable by AI agents. For its Vetted product reviews, the publisher has created Markdown versions of its content and an “MCP layer” while exploring separate presentations for booths and human readers.

Nina Gould, Forbes’ chief innovation officer, said the approach could help the publisher reach people using AI tools, but emphasized that the strategy is still developing a planned ChatGPT product-recommendation plugin that has not yet gone live. “On something like ads for agents, we’re still working on that,” she added.

Publishers are pushing back against Meta’s AI scraping terms, testing their leverage over a platform they still rely on.

Political and news creators are relied on now more than ever, but what platforms are working for them? Which ones aren’t?

The Giggly Squad deal shows podcasts have turned another corner. Video, short-form, IRL shows…where does the podcast go from here?
